PRODUCT DESIGN AND DEVELOPMENT IN MANUFACTURING

AI-Enhanced Product Design and Development at Stellar Electronics: Revolutionizing Manufacturing

Stellar Electronics is a leading player in consumer electronics. Their product design and development had long relied on traditional methods that were time-consuming and often prone to inefficiencies. Recognizing the opportunity for improvement, Rachel Miller, the head of product development, sought to incorporate AI into the design process to streamline workflows and enhance innovation. Partnering with Alex Thompson, Stellar’s chief data officer, the team began by integrating generative artificial intelligence (AI) and machine learning (ML) models to expedite product design and prototyping.

Using AI-powered design tools, Rachel’s team was able to generate multiple design iterations based on customer preferences, market trends, and historical product performance. This approach allowed them to cut the design cycle time by 30% while improving product quality and alignment with market demands. The AI system automatically generated design variations, simulated performance, and predicted customer response, enabling the team to focus on refining and selecting the most promising options.

The AI tools also revolutionized the prototyping phase. Using ML algorithms, John Lee, Stellar’s senior manufacturing engineer, simulated various manufacturing processes and material choices, optimizing for cost and efficiency. This reduced the need for physical prototypes by 25%, saving both time and resources. Stellar’s AI system accurately predicted production challenges, which allowed the team to address potential bottlenecks before entering full-scale manufacturing.

Additionally, Samantha Hayes, the director of innovation, leveraged advanced analytics to gather real-time feedback from pilot tests and customer insights. By incorporating this feedback into the AI models, the team refined product features and improved customer satisfaction rates by 20% in recent launches. The AI-driven design process enabled rapid iteration and data-backed decision making, ultimately helping Stellar bring innovative products to market faster and with greater precision.

Thanks to these AI-driven advancements, Stellar Electronics transformed its product development workflow, making it more agile, efficient, and customer-focused. The results were faster time to market and a notable increase in customer satisfaction and product innovation across its manufacturing processes.

“Innovation is the specific instrument of entrepreneurship. The act that endows resources with a new capacity to create wealth.”

—Peter Drucker, management consultant, educator, and author

In the dynamic landscape of manufacturing, the process of product design and development is the foundation of innovation and competitiveness. It plays a pivotal role in determining the success or failure of a company’s endeavors. Basically, product design and development involves the conceptualization, creation, and refinement of tangible goods and processes, ranging from consumer electronics to industrial machinery and supply chain processes. This multifaceted process involves numerous stakeholders, including designers, engineers, marketers, and supply chain professionals, collaborating to transform ideas into market-ready products. With the advent of advanced analytics, business intelligence (BI), and AI/ML technologies, product design and development has undergone a profound transformation, ushering in a new era of possibilities and challenges.

As manufacturing organizations strive to remain agile and responsive to evolving market demands, the role of data-driven decision making in product design and development has become increasingly prominent. Advanced analytics and BI empower manufacturers to leverage data insights from various sources, like customer feedback, market trends, and competitor analysis, to guide strategic product decisions. By harnessing the power of data visualization, predictive and prescriptive modeling, and trend analysis, manufacturers can gain valuable insights into consumer preferences, market dynamics, and emerging trends.

“We used AI to develop a system that advises our chemists on the next set of recipes to try as they iterate toward a final solution. We’ve found dramatic reductions, in many cases, with that approach; dramatic means reducing those R&D loops [by] 70% or 80%.”

—Chris Couch, SVP, CTO at Cooper Standard

The integration of AI/ML technologies into the product design and development process holds additional immense potential for driving innovation and efficiency. AI algorithms, such as generative design and predictive modeling, enable manufacturers to explore a vast array of design possibilities, optimize product performance, and enhance manufacturing processes. Companies like General Electric leverage AI-powered tools to accelerate product prototyping and optimize product designs for performance, cost, and sustainability.1 Rolls-Royce Civil Aerospace is using additive manufacturing to make intricate aircraft engines, thereby accelerating production.2 Chemical and life science companies are using sophisticated AI algorithms to quickly discover new chemicals and pharmaceutical products. By automating repetitive tasks and uncovering hidden insights within data, AI/ML technologies enable manufacturers to streamline the product development life cycle, reduce time to market, and foster innovation.

Despite the transformative potential of advanced analytics, BI, and AI/ML in product design and development, manufacturers still face challenges in navigating the complexities of data management, interoperability, and organizational alignment. Data silos, legacy systems, and disparate workflows hinder the seamless integration of data-driven processes into the product development life cycle, limiting the effectiveness of analytics-driven decision making. Moreover, concerns surrounding data privacy, security, and regulatory compliance add another layer of complexity, requiring manufacturers to implement robust data governance frameworks and security protocols to safeguard sensitive information and mitigate risks. The convergence of advanced analytics, BI, and AI/ML technologies presents both opportunities and challenges for manufacturers in product design and development. By embracing a data-driven approach, harnessing the power of AI/ML, and addressing challenges effectively, manufacturers unlock new avenues for innovation, enhance operational efficiency, and gain a competitive advantage in today’s rapidly evolving manufacturing landscape.

This chapter serves as a pivotal guide for manufacturers, offering insights into the intricate processes of transforming conceptual designs into tangible products efficiently and effectively. In modern manufacturing, production design and development integrates cutting-edge technologies such as AI, ML, advanced analytics, and BI into the fabric of production processes. This chapter explains how AI and ML algorithms can optimize design iterations, enhance predictive maintenance (PdM) strategies, and improve quality control (QC) measures, fostering innovation and efficiency. Advanced analytics techniques are employed to glean actionable insights from vast datasets, facilitating informed decision making at every stage of product development. BI tools provide real-time visibility into key performance indicators (KPIs), enabling proactive adjustments to production plans and resource allocation. By embracing these technologies, manufacturing practitioners can unlock new avenues for creativity, agility, and competitiveness in the ever-evolving landscape of production design and development.

FUNDAMENTALS OF PRODUCT DESIGN AND DEVELOPMENT

We now get into a comprehensive exploration of the fundamentals of product design and development, probing into the intricacies of a product life cycle. Product design and development encapsulates a multifaceted journey spanning from ideation to commercialization. This life cycle typically comprises several interconnected stages, each crucial for ensuring the success of a new product. The process typically begins with ideation, where concepts are generated based on market needs, consumer preferences, and technological feasibility. Following ideation, the design phase involves translating concepts into tangible product designs, incorporating aesthetics, functionality, and manufacturability. Prototyping then allows for the creation of physical or digital models to validate design concepts and gather feedback from stakeholders. Figure 5.1 shows a sample of the steps involved in new product design and introduction.

Key principles and methodologies serve as guiding frameworks in product design and development, offering structured approaches to problem solving and innovation. Design thinking, for example, emphasizes understanding your customers, creativity, and iterative prototyping to address user needs effectively. Agile development promotes flexibility and collaboration, enabling cross-functional teams to adapt quickly to changing requirements and market dynamics. Lean manufacturing focuses on eliminating waste and optimizing processes to streamline production and enhance efficiency. By integrating such methodologies, organizations can foster innovation, reduce time to market, and deliver products that resonate with customers.

“The design process—particularly for the prototyping phase—required several months in the past. And for this, artificial intelligence is able to support R&D because you can now virtualize your product development with AI.”

—Daniele Petecchi, Head of Data Management and AI at Pirelli

In modern product design and development, organizations face a myriad of challenges and opportunities. Rapid advancements in technology, evolving consumer preferences, and increased competition necessitate agility and innovation. Successful examples include Apple’s iterative approach to product development, evident in the continuous refinement of its iPhone lineup.3 Conversely, failures such as the Samsung Galaxy Note 7 battery explosion highlight the importance of rigorous design review and testing along with quality assurance in preventing catastrophic product defects.4 Further, globalization and supply chain disruptions underscore the need for resilient and adaptable manufacturing processes to mitigate risks and capitalize on emerging market trends.

Figure 5.1Sample process for new product design and introduction.

Figure 5.2 depicts processes within traditional product design and development. Visually, the size of the icons for computer versus human resources represents the manual versus automated processes, with most process steps either fully or partially manual. The conceptualization phase is primarily manual, with technical tool support generating product ideas through brainstorming. Similarly, conducting market research and feasibility studies is primarily manual. The items in concept development, tooling, pilot and full-scale production, and distribution are similar, with a high level of human engagement. The other processes—detailed design, planning, and post-launch evaluation—have a higher level of computer involvement and support manual efforts to achieve their goals.

The adoption of emerging technologies such as AI, ML, and advanced analytics presents new opportunities for enhancing product and process design and development. Think of these three technologies as the legs of a stool that work together to keep the stool stable. AI-driven design tools can automate repetitive tasks, optimize designs based on performance data, and generate innovative solutions that align with user preferences. ML algorithms can analyze vast datasets to identify patterns, predict market trends, and optimize product features for maximum impact. Advanced analytics provides valuable insights into customer behavior, market dynamics, and product performance, enabling data-driven decision making throughout the product life cycle.

Figure 5.2Processes within traditional product design and development.

However, when companies embark on an AI/ML or advanced analytics journey and build models to meet specific needs, there are certain things they have to keep in mind:

•First, a governance framework must be established to ensure these models adhere to well-defined governance practices, including ensuring they work in conjunction to support the company’s mission, not just a specific use case.

•Second, checks and balances need to be put in place to ensure each model is frequently monitored to ensure there is no model drift. There must be processes to:

Identify drift

Determine when and how to verify that the drift is unacceptable

Establish what to do when a drift is observed—refine the model, update it, or stop using it—all critical decisions

•Third, the company has to implement all operational processes (also called Ops—e.g., AIOps, MLOps) to ensure smooth operations of the models in the company’s standard operating procedures (SOPs). This needs to happen before each model is put into production.

•Finally, ensure the staff is trained to work with the models. Users, administrators, and developers will make or break the success of each model.

Despite the promise of advanced technologies, organizations must navigate other complexities in product design and development. Regulatory compliance, intellectual property protection, sustainability, and ethical considerations are paramount in ensuring responsible product development and commercialization. Managing stakeholder expectations, fostering a culture of innovation, and balancing competing priorities all pose significant challenges. Effective collaboration among cross-functional teams, clear communication channels, and strategic alignment with business objectives are mandatory for overcoming these challenges and driving successful product outcomes.

However, the journey of product design and development is not without risk. Failures like the Boeing 737 MAX illustrate the consequences of overlooking critical design flaws and safety considerations. In this case, rushed development, inadequate testing, and a lack of transparency contributed to tragic consequences. Conversely, success stories such as Tesla’s Model S exemplify the power of innovation and disruptive technology in reshaping industries. Tesla’s focus on electric vehicles, cutting-edge technology, and sleek design has positioned it as a leader in the automotive market, redefining the future of transportation.

Looking ahead, the future of product design and development promises continued innovation and disruption fueled by advancements in technology, ever-changing consumer expectations, and shifting market dynamics. Companies that embrace a customer-centric approach, leverage cutting-edge methodologies and technologies, and prioritize continuous improvement will thrive in this dynamic landscape. By learning from successes and failures, organizations can refine their strategies, optimize processes, and deliver products that drive value and resonate with customers. LEVERAGING ADVANCED ANALYTICS AND BI IN PRODUCT DESIGN AND DEVELOPMENT

Predictive analytics plays a pivotal role in product and process design and development. Apple exemplifies this by employing predictive analytics to forecast demand for its diverse product lineup. Through the analysis of historical sales data, consumer behavior patterns, and macroeconomic indicators, Apple accurately anticipates future demand trends and potential popular features. Apple’s BI dashboards track KPIs—such as what customers are saying on social media, customer satisfaction scores, and sales performance—enabling it to identify areas in product development for customer offerings. This has enabled Apple to optimize product design, production schedules, and inventories, and allocate global resources efficiently to improve customer satisfaction and drive sales and revenue growth. This ultimately drives operational efficiency and profitability, all through a well-designed product and process design and development.

Successful implementations of advanced analytics in product design and development are also evident in the pharmaceutical industry. Pfizer leverages advanced analytics to accelerate drug discovery and development processes. By analyzing vast amounts of both nonclinical data and clinical trial results, Pfizer identifies promising drug candidates and predicts their efficacy and safety profiles. Pfizer’s BI platforms track KPIs such as trial enrollment rates, adverse event rates, and regulatory compliance metrics. This data-driven, visual approach enables Pfizer to prioritize resources effectively, streamline regulatory approvals, and bring life-saving medications to market more efficiently. Other pharmaceutical companies like Johnson & Johnson have followed suit and utilize advanced analytics to accelerate drug development and clinical trials. These companies are analyzing genomic data, patient health records, and real-world evidence to identify potential drug targets, optimize clinical trial protocols, and predict patient responses. Additionally, they use BI platforms to track KPIs such as product performance metrics, enabling cross-functional teams to collaborate effectively and drive continuous improvement initiatives. This approach shortens the drug development timeline and enhances the likelihood of regulatory approval and market success.

Leveraging advanced analytics in product design and development is a critical strategy for manufacturers seeking to stay competitive in today’s dynamic market landscape. Advanced analytics tools and techniques enable manufacturers to gain valuable insights from various sources, including customer data, market trends, and competitor analysis. For instance, companies like Procter & Gamble (P&G) exemplify the power of advanced analytics by utilizing data from various sources, including consumer research and feedback, market trends, and supply chain dynamics. Predictive analytics has enabled P&G to introduce successful products such as Tide Pods, which revolutionized the laundry detergent market and made the products more sustainable because they used less water per load. P&G’s BI platforms tracked KPIs such as sales performance, market share, and product quality metrics to validate the success of Tide Pods. By harnessing such insights, P&G continues to make data-driven decisions throughout the product life cycle, from concept ideation to commercialization.

Companies in the automotive sector also exemplify the effective use of advanced analytics in product design. They leverage advanced analytics to analyze vast amounts of data collected from vehicles, like sensor data, telemetry data, and customer feedback. By analyzing this data, they gain insights into vehicle performance, user behavior, and preferences, which contribute to their product design decisions. They use predictive analytics to forecast demand for specific vehicle configurations, allowing the company to optimize production schedules and inventory management. Additionally, they utilize advanced analytics to identify potential design flaws or quality issues early in the development process, thus improving overall product quality and lowering the risk of costly recalls and warranty expenses. By tracking KPIs such as customer satisfaction scores and vehicle performance indicators, they can identify areas for improvement and prioritize feature enhancements to meet customer expectations. Their use of BI tools enables them to monitor real-time data on vehicle performance, battery life (for EV or hybrid cars), and customer complaints. This allows them to quickly address issues and improve overall quality through a well-designed product and related processes.

“. . . essentially, what comes in is a wish for a car, and what comes out is drawings and code. In that area, there are, of course, a lot of possibilities for data analytics and AI, both during the development process and also as part of the actual functions in the car—so intelligent functions and personalized functions and so on.”

—Anders Sjögren, Senior Technical Leader at Volvo Cars

However, the journey toward leveraging advanced analytics in product design and development is not without its challenges. The failure of Google Glass serves as a cautionary tale, highlighting the importance of understanding market needs and user preferences. Despite significant investment in technology and design, Google Glass failed to gain widespread consumer adoption due to concerns of privacy, usability, social acceptance, and product features (e.g., short battery life). This underscores the importance of conducting thorough market research, user testing, and iterative prototyping to validate product concepts and address potential barriers to adoption.

The strategic adoption of advanced analytics in product design and development offers manufacturers unprecedented opportunities for innovation and growth. By leveraging predictive analytics, companies can gain valuable insights into market trends, optimize product features, and accelerate time to market, thereby driving competitive advantage and customer loyalty. However, success requires a holistic approach that integrates data-driven decision making with market insights, user feedback, and iterative design processes to deliver products that meet the needs and expectations of customers. This has to be followed by validation processes and a commitment to quality and safety.

UTILIZING BI IN PRODUCT DESIGN AND DEVELOPMENT

In contemporary manufacturing, BI is the foundation for driving innovation and maintaining competitiveness in product design and development. By harnessing advanced analytics tools and techniques with support from BI, manufacturers gain profound insights from an array of data sources, facilitating well-informed decision making across the product life cycle. This section explores the transformative role of BI in reshaping product design and development within the manufacturing sector.

Manufacturing firms employ BI methodologies to dissect data from diverse origins, spanning customer feedback, market trends, and operational metrics, among others. Such analyses furnish invaluable inputs for shaping product design strategies, enabling companies to align their offerings with evolving consumer preferences and market dynamics. BI empowers manufacturers to discern patterns, anticipate consumer behaviors, and refine product features. Further, BI cultivates a culture of innovation within manufacturing ecosystems. By providing insights into emerging technologies, market opportunities, and competitive landscapes, BI empowers companies to embark on the development of novel products and solutions that resonate with evolving consumer demands. BI-driven innovation empowers manufacturers to outpace competitors and uphold market leadership in a swiftly evolving business milieu.

Exemplary applications of BI in manufacturing can be seen in large industrial manufacturers who harness BI tools to analyze vast amounts of operational data collected from their equipment and machinery deployed worldwide. By leveraging BI dashboards and analytics platforms, they gain real-time visibility into equipment performance, maintenance needs, and usage patterns. They also integrate BI into the engineering processes to identify inefficiencies, eliminate bottlenecks, and accelerate time to market for new products. This granular understanding of equipment and machine health enables these companies to optimize product design and development processes, enhance product reliability, and deliver superior value to customers.

To further illustrate the impact of BI on product design and development within the manufacturing sector, let’s probe into the case of a large aircraft manufacturer. It employs BI tools to analyze data generated from its supply chain, aircraft assembly lines, and maintenance operations to monitor production, identify optimization opportunities, and provide input into new product and process design. By leveraging BI dashboards and predictive analytics, the company has gained insights into component forecasts, inventory levels, and meeting customer delivery timelines. This data-driven approach has enabled it to design its products efficiently, optimize inventory, and reduce costs.

However, the journey toward leveraging BI in product design and development has challenges and pitfalls. One notable example of BI implementation gone awry is the case of a global electronics HVAC company that manufactures building automation systems. It embarked on a BI initiative to optimize its product portfolio and pricing strategies. However, due to inadequate data governance practices and siloed data sources, the BI project failed to deliver actionable insights, leading to wasted resources and missed opportunities. This cautionary tale underscores the importance of establishing robust data governance frameworks and ensuring data quality and integrity in BI initiatives.

The strategic deployment of BI in product design and development heralds promising prospects for manufacturing entities aiming to foster innovation and sharpen their competitive edge. Leveraging insights derived from diverse data sources, manufacturers can make informed decisions, optimize operational workflows, and craft products that resonate with consumer needs. Realizing the full potential of BI requires a holistic approach encompassing technological investment, organizational alignment, and a culture of data-driven decision making.

Figure 5.4 depicts the processes within modern product design and development. As you can see, compared to Figure 5.2, all the processes are at least semi-automated (as indicated by the icon size for computer and human resources), with a significantly higher contribution from computer resources compared to manual resources. Although semi-automated, most of the hard work is done by the models! These models provide guidance to human resources on actions to incorporate in operations. The manual intervention is to ensure checks and balances are in place through incorporating human-in-the-loop. This allows humans to quickly react and manually override poor outcomes from the modern tools getting pushed downstream.

Figure 5.4Processes within modern product design and development.

UTILIZING AI/ML IN PRODUCT DESIGN AND DEVELOPMENT

In the realm of manufacturing, the integration of AI and ML technologies into product design processes has become increasingly prevalent, revolutionizing traditional approaches and unlocking new avenues for innovation. At its core, AI and ML offer manufacturers a data-driven approach to product design, enabling them to harness insights from vast datasets to optimize designs, enhance performance, and accelerate time to market. By leveraging AI, ML, generative design, and advanced simulation techniques, manufacturers can streamline the product development life cycle and deliver products that meet evolving consumer needs and market demands. Typical algorithms used by companies in various sectors include predictive algorithms (e.g., regression, decision trees, time series). Also used are deep learning algorithms (e.g., convolutional neural networks, recurring neural networks, long short-term memory, generative adversarial networks, and transformer models like bidirectional encoder representations from transformers). Further, clustering algorithms like k-means and hierarchical clustering are also used along with anomaly detection algorithms like isolation forest and support vector machines.

One of the fundamental applications of AI and ML in product design for manufacturing is in the field of design optimization. Traditional design processes often rely on manual iterations and simulations to refine product designs. However, AI and ML algorithms empower designers to explore vast design spaces, identify optimal configurations, and enhance product functionality and performance. Companies utilize generative design algorithms to automatically generate innovative design concepts for components based on specified performance criteria and manufacturing constraints. These algorithms simulate thousands of design variations, iteratively refining designs to achieve optimal target metrics and ultimately leading to the production of well-engineered products.

The automotive industry has effectively leveraged AI and ML algorithms to optimize vehicle design and performance. Through the analysis of sensor data, telemetry data, and customer feedback, they have gained valuable insights into vehicle usage patterns and user preferences. These insights inform design decisions, allowing them to refine features, improve efficiency, and enhance overall customer satisfaction. For instance, the use of predictive analytics enables them to forecast demand for specific vehicle configurations, optimizing production schedules and inventory management accordingly. These predictive analytics models have enabled companies to achieve an 80% to 90% accuracy rate in forecasting demand for specific vehicle configurations, reducing inventory costs by 20% to 30%, and improving customer satisfaction by ensuring the timely availability of desired models.

AI and ML algorithms facilitate product design by enabling manufacturers to anticipate product performance, identify potential issues, and optimize design parameters before physical prototyping. AI and ML algorithms analyze historical data, simulation results, and sensor readings to predict product behavior under different operating conditions and environmental factors. For instance, automotive manufacturers leverage AI/ML models to simulate crash scenarios, assess vehicle safety, and optimize structural designs to meet stringent safety standards and regulations. By simulating crash dynamics and analyzing impact forces, manufacturers can identify potential weaknesses in vehicle structures, refine designs to enhance occupant protection, and accelerate the development of safer automobiles.

In addition to AI/ML, generative design represents a groundbreaking approach to product design that harnesses the power of AI to explore a vast array of design possibilities. Global heavy machine industry conglomerates have embraced generative design to accelerate product prototyping and optimization. By defining design constraints and performance objectives, their AI-driven design tools generate innovative solutions that optimize product performance, cost, and sustainability. For example, leveraging generative design to redesign aircraft components followed by additive manufacturing has resulted in significant weight reductions, fuel savings, and supply chain performance without compromising structural integrity and manufacturing speed. The adoption of generative design tools has led to substantial cost savings and environmental benefits.

The consumer electronics industry has mastered the art of data-driven innovation by using AI and ML. Through the analysis of consumer behavior patterns and market trends, these companies can anticipate future demand and identify potential product features that resonate with consumers. Using BI dashboards and enabling data-driven decision making at every stage of product development, they track KPIs—such as customer satisfaction scores, social media sentiment, and sales performance. This data-driven approach has enabled them to optimize product designs, production schedules, and global resources.

Large global pharmaceutical companies leverage AI, ML, and advanced analytics to accelerate drug discovery and development processes. As mentioned earlier, Pfizer uses vast amounts of data obtained with AI and ML (and advanced analytics) to identify promising drug candidates. Pfizer and other pharmaceutical companies use natural language processing tools in their pharmacovigilance initiatives. This, along with BI, helps them track adverse event rates and regulatory compliance metrics, enabling the company to prioritize resources effectively and streamline regulatory approvals. This digital approach has enabled Pfizer and others to bring life-saving medications to market faster and more efficiently, demonstrating the profound impact of AI and ML on product design and development. Moreover, their advanced analytics platform has significantly accelerated the drug discovery process, reducing time to market for new medications and improving patient outcomes. This has translated into revenue growth and improved regulatory approval rates for new drugs.

Further, the integration of AI and ML technologies into product design processes presents manufacturers with unique opportunities to optimize designs for sustainability and environmental impact. Consumer goods manufacturers have exemplified this by leveraging AI, ML, and advanced analytics to introduce eco-friendly product innovations.

P&G’s use of predictive analytics enabled the successful introduction of Tide Pods, which revolutionized the laundry detergent market. By harnessing AI and ML technologies, P&G continues to make data-driven decisions that drive sustainability and innovation in product design and development. Additionally, P&G’s use of these tools has led to a significant reduction in warm or hot water usage per load with Tide Pods, resulting in significant environmental benefits and cost savings. P&G’s goal is to help customers attain a 75% increase in cold-water washing by 2030, which will avoid having almost four million tons of CO2 released into the atmosphere. In addition, P&G is working toward moving all Tide packaging to 100% recyclable materials by 2030.5

SUMMARY AND KEY INSIGHTS

The adoption of AI and ML in product design processes necessitates a holistic approach to data governance, model monitoring, and operational management. Companies must establish robust governance frameworks to ensure that AI and ML models align with business objectives and regulatory requirements. Regular monitoring of AI models is essential to detect and mitigate model drift, ensuring that models remain accurate and reliable over time. Further, companies must implement intelligent operational processes, such as AIOps and MLOps, to ensure smooth integration and operation of AI models within SOPs. By prioritizing staff training and collaboration among cross-functional teams, companies can maximize the value of AI and ML in product design and development while mitigating potential risks and challenges.

The strategic adoption of AI and ML technologies in product design within manufacturing offers unprecedented opportunities for innovation, efficiency, and sustainability. Through the analysis of vast datasets and the application of AI, ML, predictive analytics, generative design, and advanced simulation techniques, manufacturers can optimize designs, enhance performance, and accelerate time to market. Case studies from industries such as heavy manufacturing, automotive, consumer electronics, pharmaceuticals, and consumer goods demonstrate the transformative impact of AI and ML on product design and development. By embracing a digital approach and prioritizing collaboration, companies can unlock new avenues for growth and competitiveness.

This chapter provided a glimpse into the future landscape of product design in the manufacturing sector, where AI and ML promise to catalyze transformative change. From AI-based predictive analytics and automation to enhanced supply chain visibility and optimization, manufacturers are poised to unlock new frontiers of efficiency, sustainability, and innovation. As we embark on this journey, harnessing the power of data-driven insights and emerging technologies will be imperative for manufacturers to thrive in an increasingly dynamic and competitive marketplace.

Some of the key takeaways from this chapter include:

1.Product design and development is the foundation of innovation and competitiveness in manufacturing, with key principles and methodologies serving as frameworks to guide product design and development.

2.Rapid advancements in technology, evolving consumer preferences, and increased competition necessitate agility and flexibility in product design and development. This mandates the involvement of numerous stakeholders, including designers, engineers, marketers, and supply chain professionals.

3.AI and ML can optimize design iterations, enhance PdM strategies, and improve QC measures. Similarly, advanced analytics techniques are used to glean actionable insights from vast datasets. BI tools provide real-time visibility into KPIs, enabling proactive adjustments to production plans and resource allocation.

4.By embracing cutting-edge technologies such as AI, ML, advanced analytics, and BI, organizations can foster innovation, reduce time to market, and deliver products that resonate with customers.

LEVERAGING ADVANCED ANALYTICS, BI, ML, AND AI IN PROCUREMENT

AI-Driven Procurement Transformation at Stellar Electronics: Enhancing Manufacturing Efficiency

At Stellar Electronics, the procurement process for manufacturing had long been plagued by unpredictable supplier performance, fluctuating material costs, and inventory inefficiencies. Recognizing the need for innovation, Laura Gomez, the chief procurement officer, spearheaded a project to integrate artificial intelligence (AI) and machine learning (ML) into the company’s procurement strategy. Her goal was to create a more data-driven and agile supply chain capable of responding to real-time market dynamics.

Working closely with James Yu, the senior data scientist at Stellar, Laura’s team implemented an ML platform that leveraged predictive analytics to optimize supplier selection and manage risks. By analyzing vast datasets—including historical supplier performance, pricing trends, geopolitical risks, and quality control (QC) records—the AI model identified the most reliable and cost-effective suppliers for each component. This improved supplier performance by 15% and helped reduce procurement costs by 10% within the first six months.

Furthermore, the AI system was integrated with Stellar’s material requirement planning (MRP) software. This allowed the company to better forecast material needs and dynamically adjust procurement schedules. With real-time insights, Stellar could avoid costly last-minute purchases and reduce the risk of overstocking raw materials. The predictive analytics model forecasted demand fluctuations, improving material availability by 20% while cutting inventory holding costs by 18%.

To address price volatility, Sarah Malik, the director of operations, worked with the AI team to implement automated negotiation algorithms. These algorithms analyzed historical pricing patterns and external market factors to recommend optimal purchasing windows. This allowed Stellar to secure raw materials at favorable prices, reducing overall material costs by 12% annually.

Thanks to the synergy between procurement and AI-driven insights, Stellar Electronics has drastically improved its procurement efficiency, ensuring that manufacturing operations run smoothly with minimal disruptions and optimized costs.

“With AI already being deployed in consumer applications and increasingly applied in multiple business capabilities, the number of leaders utilizing AI to transform procurement and sourcing is anticipated to dramatically increase in the coming months and years.”

—Matthias Gutzmann, Founder of Digital Procurement World

Raw material procurement is the fundamental process of acquiring essential inputs for manufacturing operations. It involves sourcing, purchasing, and managing various materials necessary for production, ranging from metals, electronics, plastics, and chemicals to natural resources. It also includes sourcing shared services such as consulting, payroll, human resources, and janitorial. Raw materials are the foundational elements upon which manufacturing industries build their products, making procurement a critical aspect of the supply chain. Services support the manufacturing processes and make sure that they work smoothly.

Efficient procurement processes are vital for the seamless functioning of manufacturing operations. Streamlined procurement ensures the timely availability of high-quality materials in the correct format, at the right place, and at the right time to minimize production delays and disruptions. Efficient procurement contributes to the company’s financials, like cost reduction, by optimizing purchasing practices, negotiating favorable terms with suppliers, and effectively managing inventory levels. By prioritizing efficiency in procurement, manufacturers can enhance operational performance, maintain product quality, and gain a competitive edge in the market. Each procurement dollar saved, whether through inventory management or payment terms, directly impacts the balance sheet and the profit and loss statement of the company.

The emergence of advanced analytics, business intelligence (BI), ML, and AI has revolutionized raw material procurement. These technologies offer manufacturers powerful tools to optimize procurement processes, improve decision making, and drive operational excellence. Advanced analytics and BI provide actionable insights by analyzing procurement data, supplier performance metrics, and market trends. By leveraging these insights, manufacturers can identify cost-saving opportunities, streamline supplier relationships, and enhance supply chain visibility. ML and AI algorithms further enhance procurement efficiency through activities such as automating routine tasks, predicting demand patterns, and optimizing supplier selection. Predictive analytics algorithms enable manufacturers to forecast raw material requirements accurately, anticipate market changes, and optimize inventory management strategies. AI-driven supplier management systems facilitate supplier selection, risk assessment, deviation from contracts, and contract negotiation, enabling manufacturers to make informed decisions and mitigate supply chain risks effectively.

In the following sections, we explore the realm of raw material procurement, exploring strategies and best practices to leverage advanced analytics, BI, ML, and AI technologies to optimize procurement processes. Through real-world experience, case studies, and practical insights, we demonstrate how manufacturers can utilize these technologies to enhance procurement efficiency, mitigate risks, and drive sustainable value creation in the manufacturing supply chain. Further, we uncover the transformative potential of data-driven procurement in the modern manufacturing landscape.

UNDERSTANDING DATA SOURCES AND INTEGRATION

Raw material procurement in the manufacturing sector relies heavily on accessing and integrating data from various sources to make informed decisions. These data sources encompass a wide range of information crucial to procurement professionals to ensure the timely acquisition of high-quality raw materials at optimal prices. Among these sources are supplier databases, market data, and internal enterprise resource planning (ERP) and manufacturing execution system (MES) software, each playing a vital role in the procurement process.

Supplier databases serve as repositories of information about potential suppliers, their capabilities, performance history, and pricing structures. By analyzing data from these databases, manufacturers can identify reliable suppliers, assess their track records, and negotiate favorable terms. Market data, on the other hand, provides insights into prevailing market trends, commodity prices, supply-demand dynamics, labor costs and availability, geopolitical factors affecting raw material availability, and weather data that can cause friction in the flow of raw material to the manufacturer. Accessing this data allows procurement teams to anticipate price fluctuations, mitigate supply chain risks, optimize sourcing strategies accordingly, and refine planning parameters to address these challenges. Internal ERP and MES systems house critical operational data, including inventory levels, production schedules, and financial records. Integrating data from ERP systems with other sources enables manufacturers to align procurement activities with production needs, minimize inventory costs, and streamline order fulfillment processes. However, despite the wealth of information available from these diverse sources, integrating and harmonizing disparate datasets pose significant challenges. Figure 6.1 shows a sample of the steps involved in raw material procurement.

“The key here is that supplier data is available where the sourcing decisions are made. This means that supplier risk and performance data has to be made available in the e-sourcing tool of the buyer so that when the supplier proposals come in, this data is taken into account to achieve a more optimized and data-based sourcing decision.”

—Nico Bac, former Senior Director of Procurement at Procter & Gamble, Founder of Digital Procurement Now

One major challenge is the heterogeneous nature of data formats, structures, and standards across different sources. Supplier databases, market reports, and ERP systems often employ proprietary formats and terminology, making it difficult to establish a common data framework for integration. Data inconsistency, duplication, and incompleteness further complicate the harmonization process, leading to inaccurate insights and decision making. Further, not all data is captured at the same level of temporal granularity, so marrying such data with others becomes challenging. To address these challenges, manufacturers must implement robust strategies for data collection, cleansing, and integration to ensure data quality and consistency across the procurement process. First, establishing data governance frameworks is essential to defining standardized data models, formats, and validation rules for all data sources. This ensures data integrity and facilitates seamless integration across systems.

Figure 6.1Sample of steps involved in raw material procurement.

Second, investing in data cleansing and enrichment technologies helps identify and rectify inconsistencies, errors, and redundancies in the dataset. Automated data cleansing tools can normalize data formats, correct inaccuracies, and enrich datasets with missing information. Adopting robust data integration platforms and middleware solutions enables manufacturers to establish seamless connectivity between disparate systems and applications. These platforms facilitate data transformation, mapping, and synchronization, ensuring smooth data flow and real-time visibility into procurement activities. However, technology by itself will not solve these problems; there must be supporting processes as well. As new technology is introduced and processes are refined, attention to educating practitioners via robust change management is critical. Remember, introducing new and improved technology without addressing the underlying processes will result in the same undesirable results manifesting themselves more quickly.

Third, leveraging advanced analytics and ML algorithms can be used to enhance data processing capabilities. Data quality is key to generating high-quality predictive insights and actionable intelligence in procurement decision making. By analyzing data such as historical procurement data, market trends, and supplier performance metrics, manufacturers can forecast demand, optimize inventory levels, and mitigate supply chain risks proactively. Fostering collaboration and data sharing among internal stakeholders and external partners promotes transparency and agility in the procurement process. By establishing data-sharing agreements, collaborative platforms, and common data quality rules, partners can exchange relevant and quality information with suppliers, customers, and industry partners, enabling better-informed decisions and fostering innovation.

Finally, ongoing monitoring, validation, and refinement of data quality and integration processes are critical to maintaining data consistency and relevance over time. Implementing performance metrics, data quality checks, and continuous improvement initiatives, along with a robust data governance framework, ensures that the procurement data ecosystem remains robust, reliable, and responsive to evolving business needs. Figure 6.2 depicts the processes within traditional procurement. As you can see, most process steps are either fully or partially manual. This is depicted by the size of the icons for computing and human resources. Remember, these are representative steps and sub-steps.

So, understanding data sources, processes, and integration is paramount for optimizing raw material procurement in the manufacturing sector. By harnessing data from multiple sources, like supplier databases, market reports, internal ERP systems, etc., manufacturers can make data-driven decisions to enhance procurement efficiency, mitigate risks, and drive competitive advantage. However, integrating and harmonizing diverse datasets requires careful planning, governance, investment in technology, and, of course, human resources. By implementing strategies for data collection, cleansing, and integration, manufacturers can ensure data quality and consistency across the procurement process. ROLE OF ADVANCED ANALYTICS AND BI IN RAW MATERIAL PROCUREMENT

Advanced analytics and BI play a crucial role in optimizing raw material procurement in the manufacturing sector. These technologies enable procurement professionals to leverage data-driven insights for informed decision making, cost optimization, and overall supply chain efficiency. By integrating advanced analytics and BI tools into procurement workflows, manufacturers can unlock numerous benefits and drive competitive advantage. These tools facilitate real-time visibility into key supply chain components like supply chain dynamics, demand forecasting, and supplier performance analysis. For instance, predictive analytics algorithms can analyze historical procurement data, market trends, and external factors to forecast raw material demand accurately. By identifying demand patterns and potential supply chain disruptions proactively, manufacturers can optimize inventory levels, minimize stockouts, and reduce carrying costs.

BI dashboards and reporting tools provide actionable intelligence by visualizing key performance indicators (KPIs), such as supplier lead times, order fulfillment rates, and material quality metrics. These insights enable procurement teams to monitor performance metrics in real time, identify bottlenecks, and prioritize strategic initiatives for process improvement.

A leading steel producer in the United States used analytics and BI to model the use and cost of scrap metal (with varied metallurgical characteristics) depending on the grade of steel to be manufactured. This effort enabled the manufacturer to increase its decision-making accuracy and reduce raw material costs by approximately 0.5%, resulting in an annual savings of nearly $10 million.1

Further, advanced analytics algorithms enhance decision-making capabilities by providing prescriptive insights for procurement optimization. For instance, sophisticated algorithms can analyze complex datasets to identify cost-saving opportunities, optimize sourcing strategies, and mitigate supply chain risks. Another large steel manufacturer implemented an optimization model to analyze historical procurement data and identify alternative suppliers offering competitive pricing and quality. As a result, it achieved a 20% reduction in raw material costs and a 30% improvement in supplier performance (e.g., lead-time reduction, on-time and in-full delivery) within six months of implementation.

Despite the significant benefits of using advanced analytics and BI in raw material procurement, several challenges exist in implementing these technologies effectively. As mentioned in the previous section, one major challenge is data quality and integration issues, stemming from disparate data sources, inconsistent data formats, and data silos within the organization. Another challenge is the complexity of analytics algorithms and the shortage of skilled personnel capable of developing and deploying advanced analytics solutions. To mitigate this challenge, manufacturers should, as a first step, work with system integrators (SIs) who are proficient in this. At the same time, manufacturers should invest in talent development programs, partner with analytics vendors, and develop knowledge-sharing initiatives to strengthen internal capabilities and expertise in advanced analytics.

Additionally, data security and privacy concerns pose significant challenges, mainly when dealing with sensitive procurement data and confidential supplier information. Manufacturers, along with their SIs, must implement robust cybersecurity measures, access controls, and encryption protocols to safeguard data integrity and protect against cyber threats and data breaches. These should be embedded within standard operating procedures (SOPs) to minimize the risk of exposing sensitive data. Resistance to change and organizational inertia are real problems and can hinder the adoption and utilization of advanced analytics and BI tools among procurement professionals. To address this challenge, manufacturers should invest in strong change management initiatives, stakeholder engagement, and training programs to foster a data-driven culture and promote user adoption of analytics solutions.

AI AND ML APPLICATIONS IN RAW MATERIAL PROCUREMENT

AI and ML are revolutionizing raw material procurement in manufacturing by enabling predictive analytics, optimization, and automation of sourcing processes. These technologies enable manufacturers to analyze vast volumes of data, identify patterns, and make data-driven decisions to optimize raw material sourcing strategies and enhance supply chain efficiency. Several AI and ML algorithms are instrumental in raw material procurement, including:

•Demand forecasting: AI and ML algorithms analyze historical sales data, market trends, and external factors to predict future demand for raw materials accurately. Improved demand forecasting can optimize inventory levels, reduce stockouts, and minimize excess inventory holding costs. For example, a large global chip manufacturer utilizes AI-driven demand forecasting to enhance its procurement processes. By leveraging advanced AI algorithms, it accurately predicts the demand for various components and raw materials required for its semiconductor manufacturing. This enabled it to optimize inventory costs by 20% (freeing up working capital), reduce lead times by 25%, and improve supplier performance to ensure the timely availability of essential materials by 25%.

•Supplier risk assessment: AI-driven risk assessment models analyze supplier performance data, market intelligence, and geopolitical factors to evaluate supplier reliability and mitigate supply chain risks. These models identify high-risk suppliers and enable proactive risk management strategies to ensure business continuity. For instance, a global manufacturing conglomerate utilizes AI-driven demand forecasting to enhance its procurement processes. By leveraging advanced AI algorithms, it has improved its risk detection accuracy by 25%, reduced supplier-related disruptions by 30%, and increased procurement cost savings by 30%.

•Price optimization: ML algorithms analyze historical pricing data, market dynamics, and demand-supply patterns to optimize raw material prices and negotiate favorable contracts with suppliers. By leveraging price optimization models, manufacturers can achieve cost savings and improve profit margins. A global consumer products company has effectively leveraged advanced AI algorithms for price optimization in procurement. It analyzes historical pricing data, market trends, supplier performance, and external factors, such as economic indicators and raw material prices. This AI-driven approach enables it to make data-informed decisions on procurement strategies, negotiate more favorable prices with suppliers, and accurately forecast future price trends. This has led to an annual procurement cost reduction of 12%. It has also reduced the supplier price variance by 15% while improving supplier performance by 10% through better quality and reliable delivery.

•Supply chain optimization: AI-powered supply chain optimization models optimize sourcing decisions, transportation routes, and inventory management strategies to minimize lead times, reduce transportation costs, and enhance overall supply chain efficiency. By dynamically adjusting procurement and logistics operations based on real-time data, manufacturers can respond rapidly to market changes and customer demands. The previously mentioned consumer products company also leverages AI to predict demand, manage inventory, optimize supplier selection, and streamline procurement processes. It reduced inventory holding costs by 15% and reduced supplier lead times by 25%, leading to a 25% decrease in stockouts by identifying and mitigating issues in the supply chain.

•Quality control: ML algorithms analyze sensor data, production records, and quality inspection results to detect defects, identify root causes, and improve product quality. By implementing AI-driven QC systems, manufacturers can reduce rework, scrap rates, and warranty claims, enhancing customer satisfaction and brand reputation. One example of a manufacturing company that has been implementing AI for QC in procurement is Flex Ltd. By analyzing data from suppliers, production lines, and historical quality issues, Flex can predict potential defects and proactively address them before they impact production. Flex has reduced its test time by about 30%, improved its product yield by 97%, and achieved an efficiency improvement of over 30%. The return on initial investment was achieved in under one month, with a return on investment (ROI) of 388%.2

Despite the transformative potential of AI and ML in raw material procurement, manufacturers face several challenges. First, as referenced earlier, data silos created by fragmented data sources and legacy systems hinder data integration and AI/ML initiatives. Manufacturers must implement data integration strategies to consolidate and harmonize disparate data sources, enabling seamless data flow and analysis. Data silos also impact data quality. Inaccurate, incomplete, or outdated data can undermine the effectiveness of AI and ML algorithms. Manufacturers must prioritize data quality management practices, including data cleansing, validation, and governance, to ensure the reliability and relevance of procurement data. Biases inherent in AI and ML algorithms can lead to unfair or suboptimal procurement decisions, perpetuating inequities or overlooking valuable opportunities. Manufacturers must assess and mitigate algorithmic biases through transparent algorithm design, diverse training data, and ongoing monitoring and validation. Resistance to change and lack of organizational readiness can impede the adoption of AI and ML technologies in procurement processes. Manufacturers must invest in change management initiatives, training programs, and stakeholder engagement to foster a culture of innovation and collaboration. Finally, regulatory requirements and data privacy concerns pose significant challenges to the adoption of AI and ML, particularly in highly regulated industries. Manufacturers must ensure compliance with data protection regulations, such as the General Data Protection Regulation (GDPR) or the California Consumer Protection Act (CCPA), and implement robust security measures to safeguard sensitive procurement data.

“Interest in AI for procurement has increased dramatically, and we expect to see a rise in use. In our survey, 58% of procurement leaders said they already are implementing or plan to implement AI in the next 12 months.”

—Kaitlynn Sommers, Senior Director Analyst at Gartner Supply Chain Practice

To mitigate these challenges, manufacturers must implement several strategies. They have to establish robust data governance frameworks to define data standards, policies, and procedures for data management, quality assurance, and security. Manufacturers can go a long way in mitigating these challenges by fostering collaboration with technology vendors, industry partners, and academic institutions to access expertise, resources, and best practices in the implementation of AI and ML. They must embrace a culture of continuous improvement and innovation by encouraging experimentation, promoting learning, and adapting to evolving technologies and market dynamics. All these are moot until manufacturers invest in workforce development initiatives to build AI and ML capabilities within the organization, including training programs, knowledge sharing, and talent acquisition. Ultimately, manufacturers must incorporate ethical considerations into AI and ML initiatives, ensuring fairness, transparency, and accountability in procurement decision-making processes. By adopting these strategies, manufacturers can harness the power of AI and ML to optimize raw material procurement processes, drive operational efficiency, and gain a competitive edge in the marketplace.

ADVANCED ANALYTICS, BI, ML, AND AI IN COST OPTIMIZATION

Advanced analytics, BI, AI, and ML play crucial roles in optimizing raw material procurement costs across diverse manufacturing sectors. These technologies enable manufacturers to analyze internal and external data to identify cost-saving opportunities. This helps them make informed decisions to streamline procurement processes. In raw material procurement cost optimization, various advanced analytics techniques and algorithms are employed, including predictive modeling, optimization algorithms, and anomaly detection.

ML-based predictive modeling is a key component of advanced analytics in raw material procurement cost optimization. By analyzing large amounts of historical data and forecasts, manufacturers have predicted future raw material prices and availability. This enables them to optimize sourcing strategies, negotiate favorable contracts with suppliers, and mitigate procurement risks. Techniques such as time series analysis, regression analysis, long short-term memory, and forecasting models are used to develop predictive models for raw material prices and demand.

Optimization algorithms are utilized to optimize sourcing decisions and minimize procurement costs. These algorithms optimize supplier selection, inventory management, transportation logistics, and production planning to achieve cost savings while meeting production targets. Techniques such as linear programming, integer programming, and constraint optimization algorithms are applied to solve complex optimization problems in raw material procurement. Anomaly detection techniques are employed to identify irregularities or deviations in procurement data that may indicate inefficiencies or fraudulent activities. By detecting anomalies in supplier invoices, purchase orders, and inventory levels, manufacturers can identify cost-saving opportunities and improve procurement processes. Techniques such as statistical analysis, ML algorithms, natural language processing (NLP), and pattern recognition are used to detect anomalies and outliers in procurement data.

In the automotive industry, predictive modeling and optimization algorithms are used to enhance sourcing decisions for critical components, reduce procurement costs, and improve supply chain efficiency. In the consumer goods sector, anomaly detection techniques are employed to identify discrepancies in supplier invoices and eliminate overpayments, leading to cost savings and process efficiencies. Further, BI tools and dashboards provide manufacturers with real-time visibility into procurement KPIs, performance metrics, and cost trends. BI platforms aggregate and visualize procurement data from multiple sources, enabling stakeholders to monitor key metrics, identify areas for improvement, and make data-driven decisions. Dashboards display metrics such as supplier performance, procurement costs, inventory levels, and lead times, allowing manufacturers to track performance and optimize procurement processes accordingly.

Despite the benefits, implementing advanced analytics, BI, AI, and ML in raw material procurement cost optimization comes with several challenges. These challenges include data integration, data quality, limited model trustworthiness, explainability of model results, change management, and regulatory compliance. Manufacturers must address these challenges by investing in data governance, data cleansing, algorithm transparency, organizational training, and compliance measures to ensure the success of their procurement optimization initiatives.

A large global manufacturer of industrial, safety, and consumer products utilizes advanced analytics and AI to optimize raw material procurement costs. By analyzing historical data and market trends, it achieved a 10% reduction in raw material costs through better negotiations with suppliers and demand forecasting accuracy.

Other examples of large companies using AI in their procurement process include a global technology, engineering, and software company that leverages BI tools and ML algorithms for cost optimization in procurement. Through data-driven insights and predictive analytics, the company has realized a 12% reduction in procurement costs and a 20% improvement in inventory turnover. Similarly, a U.S.-based technology and manufacturing company utilizes advanced analytics and AI in its procurement processes to optimize sourcing decisions and mitigate supply chain risks. By analyzing supplier performance data and market intelligence, it has achieved a 15% reduction in raw material costs and a 10% increase in profit margins. Another manufacturer of electronics and HVAC equipment uses BI tools and ML algorithms for cost optimization in raw material procurement. Through real-time data analysis and predictive modeling, the company has reduced procurement cycle times by 20% and achieved a 15% improvement in supplier performance metrics. Finally, an industrial automation and digital transformation technologies company leverages advanced analytics and AI to optimize inventory management and forecast demand for raw materials. By implementing predictive analytics models, it has achieved a 10% reduction in inventory holding costs and a 25% improvement in on-time delivery rates for critical components.

Advanced analytics, BI, AI, and ML are instrumental in optimizing raw material procurement costs across diverse manufacturing sectors. By leveraging predictive modeling, optimization algorithms, anomaly detection, and BI tools, manufacturers can achieve cost savings, improve supply chain efficiency, and gain a competitive advantage in the marketplace. However, addressing challenges such as data integration, quality, bias, and compliance is essential to maximizing the effectiveness of these technologies in procurement cost optimization.

Figure 6.4 illustrates the processes with modern procurement. As you can see, compared to Figure 6.2, most of the processes are automated—again, depicted by the size of the icons for computing and human resources. As the figure shows (along with the legend), 50% of the process steps are fully automated with the aid of advanced analytics, BI, AI, and ML. Only step 3 is fully manual, making the entire procurement process much simpler than the traditional cycle.
